I found the deeper triage problem in `plan.json`: the manual clusters exist and are enriched, but their `issue_ids` arrays are empty while the real mappings live in `action_steps[*].issue_refs` and execution-log entries. > DEVELOPER

What's the root of this problem?

> AGENT

The root problem is a broken `desloppify` state transition, not `reigh` itself.

- The scan state file is missing: [`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/state-typescript.json`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/state-typescript.json) does not exist, so `desloppify plan triage`, `status`, and `plan queue` all think there has never been a completed scan.
- The plan file still exists and is live: [`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json) still has `queue_order`, triage metadata, and workflow items. That is why `desloppify scan` says there are 7 items left and refuses to rescan.
- The saved manual clusters are partially corrupted: the current clusters still have descriptions and `action_steps`, but their `issue_ids` arrays are empty. The real membership only survives in `action_steps[*].issue_refs` and execution-log `cluster_add` entries inside [`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json). The CLI currently ignores that fallback, so it misreads the plan as having no usable manual clusters.

In short: the repo is stuck in a split-brain triage state.

- `plan.json` says “mid-triage, queue exists”
- missing `state-typescript.json` says “no scan ever happened”
- empty cluster `issue_ids` say “no manual clusters exist”

That combination is why the commands contradict each other.

> DEVELOPER

can you fix it in documents/desloppify

> AGENT

I’m fixing it in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify`. The current patch handles cluster membership falling back to `action_steps.issue_refs`; next I’m wiring the triage command to continue from a saved plan when the scan state is missing, then I’ll add tests and verify the behavior end to end.

> AGENT

I’m widening the fix slightly so the tool can actually use the repaired plan: `plan triage` will resume from saved triage metadata even when the scan file is gone, and `plan queue` will stop hard-failing in that same degraded state. After that I’m adding regression tests around the missing-scan + `issue_refs` case.

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/state.py\n@@\n def require_completed_scan(state: dict) -> bool:\n     \"\"\"Return True when the state contains at least one completed scan.\"\"\"\n     has_completed_scan = bool(state.get(\"last_scan\"))\n     if not has_completed_scan:\n         print(colorize(\"No scans yet. Run: desloppify scan\", \"yellow\"))\n     return has_completed_scan\n+\n+\n+def has_saved_plan_without_scan(state: dict, plan: dict | None) -> bool:\n+    \"\"\"Whether a saved plan can be resumed without a current scan state.\n+\n+    This is a narrow recovery path for plan/triage surfaces when ``plan.json``\n+    still contains user-authored queue/cluster metadata but the matching\n+    ``state-*.json`` file is gone.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    if state.get(\"last_scan\"):\n+        return False\n+    if not isinstance(plan, dict):\n+        return False\n+    meta = plan.get(\"epic_triage_meta\")\n+    triage_meta = meta if isinstance(meta, dict) else {}\n+    return bool(\n+        plan.get(\"queue_order\")\n+        or plan.get(\"clusters\")\n+        or triage_meta.get(\"triage_stages\")\n+        or triage_meta.get(\"strategy_summary\")\n+    )\n*** End Patch\n"
}

issue_ids: list[str]) -> None:\n+        bucket = members.setdefault(cluster_name, [])\n+        seen = set(bucket)\n+        for issue_id in issue_ids:\n+            if issue_id in seen:\n+ What works in `reigh` now:

What still does not work in the same broken state:
- `desloppify next`
- `desloppify status`
- `desloppify backlog --count 5`
- `desloppify show review --status open`

All four still return `No scans yet. Run: desloppify scan`.

So the fix is partial. It repaired the plan/triage surfaces, but not the whole product.

It is not beautiful yet. Part of it is principled:
- cluster membership is now repaired at plan-normalization time in [`migrations.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/schema/migrations.py)

But part of it is still a bandage:
- command-level recovery in [`state.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/state.py)
- special degraded-mode handling in [`workflow.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/workflow.py) and [`queue_render.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/queue_render.py)

can you fix this too while you're at it: ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

• Findings

  1. state.py:61 regresses degraded-plan recovery by dropping
     clusters[*].issue_ids and action_steps[*].issue_refs from
     _saved_plan_review_ids(). That breaks the documented helper
     contract and the existing recovery test in test_helpers.py:258:
     recover_state_from_saved_plan() now misses concerns::... IDs
     that are only retained in cluster steps. I confirmed it with
     pytest -q desloppify/tests/commands/test_helpers.py desloppify/
     tests/plan/test_schema_migrations.py, which fails on
     test_recover_state_from_saved_plan_rehydrates_queue_and_step_re
     fs.

  Assessment

  - The migration changes in migrations.py:84 make sense on their
    own:
      - normalizing hashed review IDs
      - rebuilding cluster.issue_ids from action_steps and
        execution_log
      - the new test in test_schema_migrations.py:77 is coherent
  - But that does not fully justify the state.py tightening, because
    recover_state_from_saved_plan() is a separate helper and still
    has callers/tests expecting step-ref recovery even before or
    outside migration-backed normalization.

  So the short answer is:

  - migrations.py: yes, looks coherent
  - test_schema_migrations.py: yes, good test
  - state.py: no, not as written; it introduces a real regression

  If you want, I can fix that regression now and then continue the
  queue.

For the missing-state / live-plan failure mode in `reigh`, yes, it’s working now.

I tested these here and they all run instead of dying with `No scans yet`:
- `desloppify next`
- `desloppify status`
- `desloppify backlog --count 5`
- `desloppify show review --status open`
- `desloppify plan triage`
- `desloppify plan queue --top 10`
- `desloppify plan cluster list`
- `desloppify plan cluster show edge-auth-hardening`

I also fixed the regression you pasted:
- `recover_state_from_saved_plan()` once again recovers IDs that exist only in `action_steps[*].issue_refs`
- I verified that directly with a Python assertion, and the edited modules compile cleanly

**What changed**
The fix is now mostly in the right layer, not scattered across commands:
- canonical runtime recovery in [`recovery.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/recovery.py)
- shared state loading uses it in [`persistence.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/persistence.py)
- scan gating now recognizes recovered runtime state in [`state.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/state.py)
- cluster membership is still normalized at plan-load time in [`migrations.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/schema/migrations.py)

I also removed the extra command-specific recovery logic from:
- [`workflow.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/workflow.py)
- [`queue_render.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/queue_render.py)

So: this is no longer just a `plan triage` / `plan queue` bandage. It’s not perfect, but it is materially cleaner.

**Beautiful or bandage?**
Not beautiful, but no longer hacky in the worst way.

Why it’s better now:
- recovery moved to a shared engine seam
- command surfaces now behave consistently
- cluster repair happens during plan normalization, where it belongs
- the helper-contract regression was fixed instead of papered over

Why it’s still not “beautiful”:
- recovered state is still synthetic, not a reconstructed real scan
- `status` shows honest zero scan metrics because the scan file is gone
- this is recovery logic for corrupted/missing persisted state, so some complexity is unavoidable

**Other places the same issue existed**
The same root problem affected any surface depending on `load_state()` plus `require_completed_scan()`. With the shared fix, that class is now covered, including:
- `next`
- `status`
- `backlog`
- `show`
- most `plan` subcommands
- review/resolve flows that load state directly

The other related problem was direct trust in `cluster.issue_ids`; that was broader in display/rendering code, but the plan-load normalization now restores `issue_ids`, so those callers are mostly safe as long as they use `load_plan()`.

One limitation remains: I could not run the normal `pytest` suite here because `pytest` is not installed in this environment. The verification I did was:
- `py_compile` on the changed modules
- direct Python assertions for the helper regression
- live CLI checks in `reigh`

If you want, I can do one more pass and trim any remaining duplicated recovery helpers/tests now that the canonical seam is in place.

> DEVELOPER

So how can it be beautiful?

> AGENT

Beautiful means deleting most of the special-case recovery code, not polishing it.

Right now there are two real concepts tangled together:
- plan repair
- runtime/state recovery

A cleaner design would make both explicit and one-way.

1. Canonicalize persisted data on load
- `load_plan()` should fully repair stale cluster membership and any old review-ID shapes.
- After plan load, every caller should be able to trust `cluster.issue_ids` as canonical.
- No display, queue, or triage code should need to know about `action_steps[*].issue_refs` as a fallback for membership.

2. Make runtime recovery a first-class state mode
- `load_state()` should return either:
  - real scanned state
  - recovered plan-backed state
- That should be represented explicitly, not by “missing `last_scan` but some hidden marker exists”.
- Example: a small typed runtime/status field like `state_source = scanned | recovered_from_plan`.
- Then `require_completed_scan()` and status rendering can make principled decisions without hacks.

3. Separate “open queue issues” from “historical cluster members”
- The ugliest bug came from mixing these.
- Queue/current triage should come only from `plan.queue_order`.
- Historical cluster membership can still exist in clusters/execution log.
- Those are different datasets and should be derived by different helpers, with names that make misuse hard.

4. Stop command-layer recovery branching
- `next`, `status`, `show`, `backlog`, `plan triage`, `plan queue` should not each know recovery rules.
- They should consume one normalized runtime contract.
- Command code should only render behavior, not repair persistence.

5. Add one repair command eventually
- For true beauty, recovery should not live forever as ambient magic.
- A command like `desloppify doctor repair-state` or `desloppify plan repair` could:
  - rebuild missing state from persisted artifacts where possible
  - rewrite repaired `plan.json`
  - explain what was reconstructed and what was not
- Then normal runtime paths stay simple, and recovery becomes deliberate.

So the beautiful version is:
- migration/normalization owns persisted repair
- runtime owns one explicit recovered-state mode
- commands stop carrying compatibility logic
- historical cluster membership and current open queue are never conflated again

If you want, I can turn that into a concrete refactor plan with exact seams and cleanup order.

> AGENT

Yes. The point is to keep old persisted data working, not to make commands “special”.

Your preference is the right one:
- allow data compatibility shims at load/migration boundaries
- do not allow command/runtime functionality shims that change behavior per command

That means the target design is:
- old or damaged `plan.json` / state payloads get normalized into one canonical in-memory model
- every command consumes that canonical model
- no command knows about “missing scan file but surviving plan”
- no `require_completed_scan()` exceptions based on hidden recovery flags

> DEVELOPER

could this be fundamentally simplified or improved - consider it

> AGENT

Yes. The clean simplification is to collapse this into two layers only:

- `load_plan()` repairs legacy plan data and makes `cluster.issue_ids` canonical.
- `load_state()` returns a canonical state model with explicit capabilities like “issue inventory available” and “scan metrics available”.

Then the rest gets smaller:
- delete hidden recovery markers
- replace `require_completed_scan()` with capability checks
- let commands consume one normal state shape
- keep a single explicit repair command to persist the reconstructed state when the user wants to eliminate legacy/corrupt artifacts

I’m implementing toward that shape now rather than extending the current recovery branchwork.

> AGENT

I’ve got the simplification target pinned down. The implementation is going to be: canonical `scan_metadata` on state load, no hidden recovery marker, a pure inventory gate for commands, and a real `plan repair-state` command that persists the reconstructed state instead of relying on ambient magic forever.

> AGENT

Implemented.

The main change is that missing-scan recovery is now data-shaped instead of command-shaped. State uses explicit `scan_metadata` rather than a hidden `_saved_plan_recovery` flag, command gating is inventory-based via [`state.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/state.py), plan-derived reconstruction stays in [`recovery.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/recovery.py), and the narrative no longer lies about a “first scan complete” when the tool is running from reconstructed plan data in [`headline.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/narrative/headline.py). I also added coverage for the recovered-state headline and the persisted repair path in [`test_saved_plan_recovery.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/commands/plan/test_saved_plan_recovery.py) and [`test_recovered_state_headline.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/narrative/test_recovered_state_headline.py).

Verification is solid. I ran `113` targeted tests in the repo’s `.venv`, all passing. I also ran the real CLI against `reigh`, and these now work in the broken missing-state scenario: `next`, `status`, `backlog --count 5`, `show review --status open`, `plan triage`, `plan queue --top 10`, `plan cluster list`, and `plan cluster show edge-auth-hardening`. `status` now reports reconstructed inventory honestly instead of claiming a completed scan.

One remaining wart: `require_completed_scan()` is still present as a compatibility name, but it now aliases the canonical inventory gate. The behavior is right; the name is legacy. I did not run `desloppify plan repair-state` in `reigh`, because that would write a new `.desloppify/state-*.json` there; the command itself is covered in tests. There is also an unrelated existing worktree change in [`execution_summary.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/execution_summary.py) that I left alone.

> AGENT

Yes. I cleaned up the non-data compatibility in this path.

The meaningful simplification is in the state boundary:
- [`schema.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/schema.py) now treats `scan_metadata.source` as the single source of truth. Canonical normalized state no longer persists redundant `inventory_available` / `metrics_available` flags.
- Older saved payloads with those flags still load, but they are compatibility input only now.
- Recovery/runtime code reads explicit helpers instead of poking raw dict fields in [`recovery.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/recovery.py), [`persistence.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/persistence.py), and [`repair_state.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/repair_state.py).

I also removed broad compatibility-facade usage from the touched command surfaces:
- status/runtime/helper paths now use [`state_io.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/state_io.py) and [`state_scoring.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/state_scoring.py) directly instead of routing through `desloppify.state`

> AGENT

I checked `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify`. The root problem is a split triage state model, not bad data in `reigh`.

`apply_completion()` marks triage complete by:
- purging `triage::*` queue items
- recording `triaged_ids`, `strategy_summary`, and `last_completed_at`
- archiving stage reports into `last_triage`
- clearing live `triage_stages`

That happens in [helpers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/helpers.py#L332).

The queue/lifecycle path treats that state as complete. `sync_triage_needed()` decides whether triage is stale from `triaged_ids` and the review snapshot hash, so `desloppify next` correctly moved on to execution work in [triage.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/sync/triage.py#L120).

But the `desloppify plan triage` dashboard does not use that same status model. It reads only `meta["triage_stages"]`, and when that dict is empty it falls into “start with observe” guidance:
- [dashboard.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/display/dashboard.py#L253)
- [layout.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/display/layout.py#L87)

So there are two incompatible definitions of “where triage is”:
- lifecycle/queue: archived completion is complete
- dashboard: empty live stages means start again

That is why `next` was right and `plan triage` misled me.

The deeper design issue is:
- no single canonical triage-status helper
- display code bypasses lifecycle/staleness policy
- tests cover completion cleanup, but not the post-completion dashboard state

The right fix is to make the dashboard render from the same canonical completion/staleness decision the queue uses, instead of inferring status from `triage_stages` alone.

> AGENT

The live `reigh` queue is a 12-cluster execution sequence, and the front of it is coherent:

1. `edge-security-and-eligibility`
2. `runtime-init-and-user-settings`
3. `shared-core-boundaries`
4. `generation-repository-contracts`
5. `travel-structure-migration`
6. `edge-handler-hygiene-and-splitting`
7. `composition-assembler-decomposition`
8. `lightbox-boundaries-and-task-details`
9. `lora-and-shared-ui-boundaries`
10. `image-form-and-upload-contracts`
11. `ui-foundation-and-hygiene`
12. `test-gap-closure`

That top 12 feels mostly thoughtful. It starts with security and runtime correctness, then shared boundaries and canonical contracts, then dependent decompositions, then hygiene/UI cleanup, and leaves tests until the contract churn settles. That arc makes sense.

But the full saved queue is not uniformly thoughtful yet.

What’s good:
- The active 12-cluster execution order is coherent.
- The top cluster action steps are concrete and executor-ready. The ones I checked have file anchors, issue refs, and effort tags.
- The strategy summary and the front of the queue line up.

What’s weak:
- Only 18 of 63 manual clusters have an explicit `priority`; 45 manual clusters still have no priority at all.
- Action steps do not carry explicit step priority or step dependencies anywhere. They are just ordered lists with `effort`, `detail`, and `issue_refs`.
- The older backlog still contains many broad, stale, or mixed-purpose clusters like `structure-and-package-org`, `naming-and-small-fixes`, `frontend-error-handling`, `lightbox-hooks-cleanup`, etc. Those are usable, but they do not feel like the result of one clean end-to-end prioritization pass.
- `plan cluster list` still interleaves new deliberate clusters with older opportunistic ones like `exports-codemod` and `boilerplate-removal`, which makes the overall plan feel less curated than the top execution path.

So the honest answer is:
- the front of the queue: yes, reasonably thoughtful
- all action plans prioritized: no
- the whole backlog: no, not yet

The current plan is good enough to start execution at the top. It is not yet a beautifully prioritized global backlog. The next cleanup would be to normalize the long tail:
- assign priorities to all remaining manual clusters
- split or retire the broad legacy buckets
- add explicit dependency metadata where ordering actually matters
- keep hygiene/codemod work from competing visually with root-cause contract clusters

> AGENT

No. The front of the queue is thoughtful; the whole backlog is not.

Right now `next` shows only the 12 live execution clusters I listed earlier. It does not show all 18 explicitly-prioritized clusters, and it definitely does not show the 44 active manual clusters that still have no priority. Operationally, those are “at the back,” but not in a very clean way. They still exist in the saved cluster backlog and can confuse `plan cluster list`, even though they are not on the live `next` path.

The main weak clusters are the broad, overlapping, or stale ones:
- `structure-and-package-org`
- `naming-and-small-fixes`
- `lightbox-hooks-cleanup`
- `frontend-error-handling`
- `edge-function-error-and-auth`
- `incomplete-migrations`
- `dead-code-deletion`
- `convention-standardization`

Some are worth doing, but not in their current form. `structure-and-package-org`, `lightbox-hooks-cleanup`, `edge-function-error-and-auth`, and `incomplete-migrations` are real work, but they overlap newer root-cause clusters and should be merged/split, not executed blindly. Some are mostly opportunistic cleanup and should stay late: `naming-and-small-fixes`, `commentary-thinning`, `hardcoded-colors`, `boilerplate-removal`, `exports-codemod`. Some are partly stale already: `naming-and-small-fixes`, `frontend-error-handling`, and `dead-code-deletion` contain “already fixed / verification-only” steps, which is a sign the backlog was repaired enough to validate, not fully re-authored.

The poorly-engineered thing would be to execute overlapping old clusters alongside the new root-cause ones. The worst overlaps are:
- `lightbox-hooks-cleanup` with `lightbox-boundaries-and-task-details`
- `travel-structure-contract` / `travel-contract-unification` with `travel-structure-migration`
- `generation-task-seam` / `generation-ta[REDACTED_SK]` with `generation-repository-contracts`
- `runtime-singletons-and-bootstrap` / `settings-and-bootstrap-singletons` with `runtime-init-and-user-settings`
- `edge-function-error-and-auth` / `edge-auth-hardening` / `edge-request-and-auth-surface` with `edge-security-and-eligibility` and `edge-handler-hygiene-and-splitting`

One more important mismatch: 6 older clusters still have explicit priority labels but do not show in `next`:
- `exports-codemod`
- `boilerplate-removal`
- `shared-components-reorg`
- `test-clipPending-segmentOutputs`
- `hook-decomposition`
- `task-params-typing`

So the priority metadata is not fully trustworthy globally. `next` is the thing to trust right now, and `next` currently surfaces only the 12-cluster execution queue.

If you want, I can do one cleanup pass on the backlog itself next:
- retire stale clusters
- merge overlapping ones into the 12 live clusters
- assign explicit priorities only where they still matter
- remove the misleading old P1/P2 cleanup clusters from the serious execution story

I went through it and cleaned the plan substantially.

In `reigh`, the manual backlog is now just the 12 live execution clusters. I removed the 51 detached historical manual clusters that were no longer attached to the current queue, so `plan cluster list` is no longer mixing the real backlog with stale old planning artifacts. I also narrowed `ui-foundation-and-hygiene` and permanently skipped two low-value churn items:
- `review::.::holistic::convention_outlier::react_style_islands_in_ui_subsystems`
- `review::.::holistic::ai_generated_debt::comment_banners_on_trivial_surfaces`

That leaves `46` open review issues, down from `48`, and the live queue is still a coherent 12-cluster sequence. The first item is still `edge-security-and-eligibility`, and the late clusters now read more honestly:
- `ui-foundation-and-hygiene` is explicitly late-phase and no longer implies a repo-wide React.FC churn pass
- `test-gap-closure` is explicitly late-phase follow-up after form/timeline contracts settle

I also encoded the dependency chain into the saved plan data under `depends_on_clusters` in [plan.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json), even though the current CLI views do not render that field. The key links now in the file are:
- `edge-handler-hygiene-and-splitting` after `edge-security-and-eligibility`
- `generation-repository-contracts` after `shared-core-boundaries`
- `image-form-and-upload-contracts` after `generation-repository-contracts`
- `composition-assembler-decomposition` after `travel-structure-migration`
- `lightbox-boundaries-and-task-details` after `composition-assembler-decomposition`
- `lora-and-shared-ui-boundaries` after `lightbox-boundaries-and-task-details`
- `ui-foundation-and-hygiene` after `lora-and-shared-ui-boundaries` and `image-form-and-upload-contracts`
- `test-gap-closure` after `image-form-and-upload-contracts` and `composition-assembler-decomposition`

I verified with live CLI commands:
- `desloppify plan queue --top 20`
- `desloppify plan cluster list`
- `desloppify next`
- `desloppify plan cluster show ui-foundation-and-hygiene`

Backup is at [plan.json.backlog-curation.20260311_044556.bak](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json.backlog-curation.20260311_044556.bak).

One caveat: `plan cluster list` / `plan cluster show` do not currently display the saved dependency metadata, even though it is present in the plan file. > DEVELOPER

so it's code-money proof?

> AGENT

Not fully, but much closer.

If you mean “safe to spend real engineering time on without drifting into vanity cleanup,” then mostly yes for the manual queue now:
- the live backlog is down to 12 substantive clusters
- overlapping historical clusters were removed
- two low-value churn issues were permanently skipped
- the remaining steps are concrete and mostly root-cause oriented

What is not “proof” yet:
- dependency links are stored in the plan, but the CLI does not really enforce or display them well
- the auto-generated clusters are still noisy in the background
- a future rescan can still reintroduce broad mechanical debt that needs judgment

So:
- manual triage backlog: mostly worth doing now
- entire `desloppify` experience: not fully foolproof yet

The main remaining product gap is in `Documents/desloppify`, not `reigh`: the tool should render and honor the dependency graph explicitly so the good prioritization is enforced, not just saved.
